# Aliases — finding an article under the name you looked it up by

The 1913 encyclopedia does not always file an article under the word a reader
would search for. *Marriage* is **Sacrament of Marriage**; *Incarnation* is
**The Incarnation**; *Mass* is **Sacrifice of the Mass**; *Charity* is
**Charity and Charities** for the works and **Love (Theological Virtue)** for
the virtue. Grep for the headword and you find nothing, which looks like a gap
in the corpus and is not.

This file is the map. It has three parts and they are **not** equally
authoritative.

1. **The encyclopedia's own cross-references** — 10 of them, entries the
   1913 work prints as `Abbacy. See Abbot.` and Wikisource transcribes as
   one-line pages. This is the work telling you where it filed the subject, and
   it is the best evidence in this file.
2. **Curated** — 36 headwords, checked one at a time against the catalogue
   while building this corpus. Where the encyclopedia prints its own *see*
   reference the note says so. Trust these.
3. **Wikisource redirects** — 1550 of them, made by the editors who
   transcribed the work and read out of the API. Somebody's judgement about
   what a reader would look up, and reliable.
4. **Derived** — 2380 produced mechanically here by stripping a leading
   article or qualifier, un-inverting a comma, or dropping a parenthesis. These
   are **ours and they are guesses**. The rule picks the alphabetically first
   article that fits, which is right for *Aachen, Diocese of* → *Aachen* and
   wrong for *Marriage* → *History of Marriage* — which is why the curated
   layer exists and takes precedence. A derived alias is a search aid, never a
   citation.

Generated by `99. Tools/encyclopedia/convert_cathen.py`. Do not hand-edit; add
to `CURATED` in the converter instead.
